# Divergence register

Every deliberate difference between the Rust port and the C++ reference.
The owner reviews this register at each gate and signs it off in work
package P7.1. See [verification.md](verification.md) for when an entry is
required.

Reasons are one of: *undefined behaviour in the reference*, *legacy
platform dependence*, *numeric policy* (decision T11), *owner decision*
(cite its ID), or *technical decision* (cite its ID; added 2026-11-17).

The owner accepted D11 on 2026-11-17. On the same day the owner accepted
D87 to D117 and D150 to D156: the rows that replace undefined behaviour of
the reference as a class, and the design rows one by one (D87 to D93, D95,
D96, D98, D99, D107, D117 and D156).

The owner accepted D118 to D128 (P2.4) on 2026-11-17: D124 to D126 as open
gaps that P3.3 removes (turret aiming and gun lock may come with P2.5);
D122 and D123 to be revisited when multi-part ships and golems need them;
D120 removed before P2.4 merged, once the P2.1 `ClassifyPoint` was on
`main` (the ID is not reused); the rest as design choices.

The owner accepted D190 to D192 (P2.5) on 2026-11-18, as replacements of
undefined behaviour in the reference.

The owner accepted D160 to D163, D170 to D173 and D193 to D196 (P2.5) on
2026-11-18: D195 on the condition that the world delivers the queued
notifications after the tick in the order the reference delivers them
during it (done on `claude/p2.5-goto`); D174 removed (its gaps are open
items of the ledger's AI sub-row; the ID is not reused).

The owner accepted D180 to D183 (the shared remainder of P2.4) on
2026-11-18. On the same day D124 was removed (turret joints and the lock
clock are driven now; what is left is D183; the ID is not reused) and D128
was narrowed to the power pass.

The owner accepted D184 and D185 (P2.5 loot and flee) on 2026-11-18.

The owner accepted D157 to D159, D168 and D169 (P2.5 escort: the follow,
formation and guide behaviours) on 2026-11-19. D159 (the base follower's
dock delegate got no `DirectiveDockOp` and no offset) was removed when the
dock behaviour gave the delegate its dock directive and offset (branch
`claude/p2.5-dock`); the ID is not reused.

The owner accepted D175 to D177 (the guided-missile controller, moved
from P3.3 into P2.4's remainder) on 2026-11-18, and the narrowing of D125
to the mines and counter-measures. D177 falls under the rule for fights
decided the same day: a fight is judged exactly up to its first contact or
munition impact, and after it within the Gate G2 envelopes.

On 2026-11-19 the owner decided that a fight's first contact or impact
must come in the oracle's tick, as `ram_ship`'s contact must. D177's port
rule was corrected for it (branch `claude/g2-strafe`): the reference
evaluates a phantom's mindists in the PSI after the pose, so a body
overlapping at the end of one step is an intruder in the next, without
the second overlapping step the earlier rule asked for; `strafe`'s missile
is spent in the oracle's tick with it. The owner accepted the amended wording
of D177 and the new row D179 (the death fuse that keeps `strafe`'s hauler
in space, until the server's fuses are ported in P3.3) on 2026-11-19.

On 2026-11-19 the owner accepted the amended wording of D91: the port's ray queries now apply IVP's OV sphere prefilter and its start-inside rule (branch `claude/g2-shield-hit`), which removed the early shield hits of four G2 ensemble seeds.

The owner accepted D188 and D189 (P2.4 asteroid fields) and the narrowing
of D181 to the reference's null reads on 2026-11-19. On the same day the owner decided D198: the world loads
asteroid fields whatever form the data path has.

ID ranges, reserved on 2026-11-18 so that work packages running in
parallel do not collide: IDs below D200 stay with Phases 2 and 3; D200 to
D299 belong to Phase 4. A work package takes the next free ID of its
range.

the reference's does. D346 stays as accepted: the cursor keeps its
pre-transformed quad. D481 is narrowed: on the owner's decision the

different files of one name in several folders); what remains, the
holder's archetype reference, has no effect and is accepted by the same
rule.

In a fifth review on 2026-11-19 the owner accepted D490 to D495 (P4.8,
third increment: the particle and mesh appearances and the client's mesh
factory; D490 by the owner's choice, so mesh appearances load the meshes
the data names and the trade lane tube shows its mesh, which the reference
loses to its `(null)` path), D500 and D501 (P4.7, third increment:
instance animations and aim events through the engine), D510 to D513
(P4.13, seventh increment: the engine's interface pass), D520 to D528
(P4.10, the client wiring: the sound archetypes, voice profiles, play keys
and sound tick through the engine), D530 to D534 (P4.1 and P4.11: the
account derivation of a new install, `PerfOptions.ini`'s options and
display mode, and the low-machine warning), D540 to D546 (P4.14, saving a
scene and the script engine's COM context and file-system paths), D550 to
D555 (P4.11 to P4.13, the IME input box and font), D560 to D562 (P4.12 and
P4.14: the script engine's custom load parser, and the rich-text window's
own scroll bar as a `UIScroll` child) and D570 and D571 (P4.4, P4.5,
P4.12 and P4.16, the render residuals). D461 and D422 are closed:
`Engine::play_sound` plays through the client's playing list, the frame's
sound tick and the mixer's tick, and `BaseRichTextWin` makes its bar as a
`UIScroll` child and hears its value through slot 23, as the reference
does; their rows stay for their history.

Narrowed with these increments: D460 (the models' animations play on
their own lists, with their sinks and aim events; what remains is that
the engine, not the game's character objects, advances them; accepted in
the fourth review, the narrowed row stays accepted); D284 (the window
holds the composition at the focus loss and cancels it, so the box
receives it once; what remains is that it arrives after the pump); D283,
D285, D286 and D471 (the game draws the IME's toolbar beside the focused
box, and its composition and candidate drawing is ported for what an IME
context hands the game, which the window's does not; the client's boxes
measure with their own font; the edit window hands its box the UTF-16
unit; `FLImeInputBox` is ported). D475's Rust column names both bars,
which take the manager's numbers.

With P4.11's fourth increment (`imeui`'s support level 3, readings and
version words, for the messages an IME context hands the game) D590 to
D593 are proposed. D283 is narrowed: the inline Korean composition of
level 3, the reading window and the version word are ported for what an
IME context supplies (`text_input::ime_core`); what remains is that the
window's context, under T7, hands no messages or readings (it names the
IME's file for the version word). D552 is narrowed: the
version word is `GetImeId`'s and the rules of the Chinese IMEs it
recognises are taken; the orientation also follows the layouts the
messages see.

The owner deferred the VMesh-backed deformable draw (P4.6's
`DeformServices` on the stack, P4.7's VMesh part of a VMesh-backed mesh)
on 2026-11-19, as `TriMesh`'s editing mode (D358): no shipped model has

| ID | Area | C++ behaviour | Rust behaviour | Reason | Observable effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | `neuron-core::ini::engine` | On a stray `]` or `=` at the start of a line, an over-long phrase, or a value that does not end the line, `FIniDatabase::Parse` executes `__debugbreak`; on a key before the first section it writes through a null section (`neuroncomponents.cpp`). Without a debugger the process ends | `EngineIni::load_*` returns `EngineIniError::Breakpoint` or `KeyBeforeSection`; the sections parsed before stay | Undefined behaviour in the reference | A malformed engine INI is reported instead of ending the program |
| D2 | `neuron-core::ini::engine` | The lexer copies a word into a 1024-byte buffer without a bound; a longer word overwrites the length that follows it | A word may be any length; the phrase limits then apply | Undefined behaviour in the reference | None for words up to 1024 bytes |
| D3 | `neuron-core::ini::engine` | `@include` nests without a limit; an include cycle overflows the stack | Nesting deeper than 64 returns `EngineIniError::IncludeTooDeep` | Undefined behaviour in the reference | An include cycle is reported instead of crashing |
| D4 | `neuron-core::ini::engine` | `_splitpath` on an `@include` name whose directory part is 256 bytes or more calls the invalid-parameter handler, which ends the process | The path is composed as for shorter names | Undefined behaviour in the reference | None for directory parts under 256 bytes |
| D10 | `neuron-core::ini::reader` | `INI_Reader::open` decodes a file that starts with a binary INI header (`common_ini.cpp`) | Every file is read as text | Owner decision O13 | None for the game data, which has only text INI files |
| D11 | `neuron-core::math`, `net::wire::quantise`, `util::spline` | The x87 precision control rounds float arithmetic: 24 bits on the client's main thread after Direct3D 8 `CreateDevice`, 53 bits on the dedicated server and in the oracle (`Directory.Build.props`) | Always the 53-bit result: `f64` arithmetic, rounded to `f32` at each store the reference makes to a `float` | Numeric policy (T11) | Client-side math, quantised wire values and spline results can differ from the single-player C++ client in the last bits; a product within about 2^-24 of an integer can quantise one step lower |

The Content reactions to the dispatched messages come with P3.5c (missions, `AbortMission`), P3.6e (BaseSupport: the dealers' `GFGoodBuy`, `GFGoodSell`, `GFObjSelect`) and P3.7c (the vendor and bar offers' `MissionResponse`) | Work in progress; accepted by the owner on 2026-11-25 | A client's player-state and trade requests do nothing yet on a dedicated server |

| D920 | `game-logic::session` (P3.9) | A refused `BaseExit` or `LocationExit` pushes a null packet to the client (`PlayerData_SendClientNullPacket`, `IServerImpl.cpp:285-288`); what the multiplayer transport does with it is not recovered | Nothing is sent | Undefined behaviour in the reference; accepted under decision O48 (3) and confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-25 | None with a client that follows the protocol |
| D931 | `game-logic::receive` (P3.10a) | `IServerImpl::MineAsteroid` calls `spawn_mined_loot` on `SysDB_LookupSystem`'s result without a null test (`IServerImpl.cpp:1914-1919`) | A system the server does not have spawns nothing | Undefined behaviour in the reference; accepted under decision O48 (3) and confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-25 | None with a client that names its own system |
| D932 | `game-logic::{world, cargo}` (P3.10a) | A client's munition report names the munition archetype `StarSystem::munition_collision` reads through `Archetype::GetEquipment` without a null test (`server_classes.cpp:9218-9231`), and `EqObj::slot_58` reads the used stack's archetype the same way (`server_gameobject.cpp:2287`) | An archetype the data lacks deals no damage (the world's `munition_collision_hooked` returns) or makes the use refused (-5) | Undefined behaviour in the reference; accepted under decision O48 (3) and confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-25 | None on the shipped data: the clients' munitions and cargo are its archetypes |
| D933 | `game-logic::receive` (P3.10a) | `GoTradelane` and `StopTradelane` compare the ship with `Players[Session]`'s raw slot without `PlayerDB::is_valid` (`IServerImpl.cpp:530-556`), reading past the table for a session above the limit | A session without a slot is refused | Undefined behaviour in the reference; accepted under decision O48 (3) and confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-25 | None: the host gives sessions within the limit |

D5 to D9 described the reader's binary INI path. That path was removed
with decision O13, so the rows are gone; the IDs are not reused. D32
described the directory `FindFirst` passing a partial path to the system;
decision O14 replaced that code, so the row is gone too.

The reference's save writer was changed on purpose (decision O12): it
writes plain text instead of `FLS1`. The port matches the changed
reference, so that change is not a divergence here.
